# Every comment in DungeonMaster

Generated by `npm run comments`. 5,243 comments, 5,457 lines of them, across 453 of 480 source files, in folder order.

Do not edit: this file is written from the source and any change here is lost.

_22 comments_


### deploy/bootstrap.sh


**L1–6**

> !/usr/bin/env bash
> One-time setup for the three dnddungeon.com origins. Safe to re-run.
>
>   bash ~/dm-bootstrap.sh --target-ip <addr> [--deploy-key "ssh-ed25519 AAAA...== ci-deploy"]
>
> Releases live at /opt/dungeonmaster/<name>/releases/<timestamp>-<sha>/.


**L14**

> Ports must agree with the deploy workflow's port list and the reverse proxy's vhosts.


**L17**

> -gt 0 ]; do


**L30**

> The address CI connects to, checked against this host's own.


**L42**

> Caddy holding the ports is the expected state on a re-run.


**L71**

> Owned by the deploy user, readable by `caddy`: 755, not 750.


**L83**

> `current` is resolved per request, so it must point somewhere from the start.


**L102**

> Not a real sha256, so the first deploy after bootstrap always builds.


**L112–113**

> /etc/caddy/Caddyfile becomes a stub that imports /etc/caddy/conf.d/*.caddy, one file per project.
> Any existing config is moved rather than rewritten.


**L121**

> `import` at the start of a line marks the stub.


**L123**

> Only preserved if the file declares a site.


**L141–142**

> No site blocks, only the import. Each project writes its own file under conf.d.
> Written by DungeonMaster/deploy/bootstrap.sh.


**L148–149**

> Overwritten on every run; edit this in the repo, not on the host.
> Site addresses are port-only (":8081"): no automatic HTTPS, and any Host matches.


**L151**

> The blocks differ only in port and root.


**L159**

> "Is Caddy alive", independent of whether a release is present.


**L167**

> /_next/static is fingerprinted and cached forever; index.html and the stamps never are.


**L174**

> `output: 'export'` emits /format.html for /format, so the .html arm is required.


**L179**

> The app's own 404 page, served with a real 404 status.


**L187**

> Drop the leading blank line and the trailing newline, so `caddy validate` reads it as formatted.


**L200**

> The same public key must be here even when CI connects through a jump host.


**L212**

> `|| true`, not `|| echo 0`: grep -c already prints "0" on no match.


**L232**

> Step 6 moved any pre-existing config to a new path.

## `mods/editor/morale_studio-6fdec9ac2a038c2c`

_7 comments_


### mods/editor/morale_studio-6fdec9ac2a038c2c/main.js


**L1–13**

> Morale (studio half).
>
> The authoring side of a paired mod: this adds a `morale` field to every
> monster, and `mods/engine/morale-*` reads it during play. Neither half needs
> a schema change, because the value lands in the monster's `extra` bag —
> which `schema/common.ts` documents as the supported way to exceed what the
> format ships with.
>
> The practical consequence: a module carrying `extra.morale` still validates,
> still compiles, and still hashes stably against a stock engine. Someone
> without these mods can open and play the game; they just do not get morale.


**L15**

> Extra fields on whatever the author has selected.


**L20**

> Only for a monster: `content.monsters` then an index.


**L41**

> Extra validation, surfaced in the problems console beside the engine's own.


**L71**

> A bulk action, offered in the Mods dock.


**L73**

> Without `run` this is the "what can you do" question.


**L96**

> A panel: what the module looks like through this mod's eyes.

_4 comments_


### mods/engine/morale-bf33bf71f9aa8b6b/main.js


**L1–10**

> Morale (engine half).
>
> Reads the `extra.morale` its studio half writes and makes it mean something:
> a monster whose vitality drops below its morale threshold turns and runs.
>
> The pairing is the point. Neither half knows about the other's code; they
> agree only on a path into a monster's `extra` bag, which is data both the
> schema and a stock engine already tolerate.


**L13**

> Only worth looking after something that could have hurt someone.


**L28**

> The threshold lives on the statblock the author edited in the studio.


**L35**

> Ten is "never runs"; zero is "runs at the first wound".

## `mods/engine/thorns-5422f9a9bca0a023`

_13 comments_


### mods/engine/thorns-5422f9a9bca0a023/main.js


**L1–12**

> Thorns.
>
> Taking a wound while standing in briar leaves thorns in you. Three or more and you cannot settle
> enough to rest; waiting picks one out.
>
> The briar check is the point: it reads the terrain under your feet, so getting bitten on dry
> stone costs you nothing.
>
> It also uses every hook and directive kind the mod system has, which is why the test suite drives
> this mod rather than a synthetic one.


**L14**

> Thorns at or above this and rest is off the table.


**L17**

> The terrain that does it. Anything tagged thorny would do; this is greenmarch's.


**L24–28**

> Is the given entity standing in briar? `TileMap` is a flat row-major array of terrain ids, so the
> tile under a position is `tiles[y * width + x]` — the same arithmetic `terrainAt` does inside the
> engine.


**L42–46**

> A wound taken in the briar leaves something behind. `event.emit` has to name the event type it
> wants — an unfiltered declaration would put a sandbox crossing on every event in the game — so
> this is declared `match: "damaged"`.


**L50**

> Only what the player is controlling: a hound bleeding in the same thicket is not something the


**L51**

> party has to pick out later.


**L63**

> Carrying too many, and you cannot settle.


**L68**

> The refusal reads in this mod's own words: every key here is resolved from this mod's


**L69**

> `systemText`, not the engine's table.


**L73**

> Waiting picks one out — the way back down.


**L91–95**

> `pluckThorns`, an effect op the engine has never heard of. Declared `replace`, so this stands in
> for the engine's unknown-op refusal, and any module's JSON can use `{ "op": "pluckThorns",
> "amount": 2 }` with no core change.


**L107**

> Resting is when you would notice them all gone.

> Only creatures above this intellect learn; beasts never do.


**L95**

> What they learn to counter.


**L99**

> Adaptations decay too, if the party stays away long enough.


**L105**

> A named override the GM can drop on any of the above.


**L111**

> Which deeds it applies to; empty means all of them.


**L114**

> Force these NPCs to know it, regardless of witnessing or distance.


**L116**

> These never learn of it, whatever the simulation says.


**L118**

> Override the global spread and decay for these deeds.


**L122**

> Skip the simulation entirely for these deeds.


**L130**

> Who drives propagation: the engine, the content, or both.


**L136**

> Targeted overrides, applied in order after the global settings.


### packages/module/src/schema/module.ts


**L1**

> The module document: an entire game as one JSON file.


**L11**

> The module format's own version, bumped when the schema breaks compatibility.


**L20**

> Free-form, for the editor's gallery.


**L25**

> How a character is built at the start of a game.


**L28**

> Points to spend across attributes.


**L30**

> Cost of raising an attribute to each value; index is the target score.


**L34**

> Restrict the choices offered; empty means everything is allowed.


**L50**

> How many characters the player controls.


**L53**

> Where play begins: a region for story mode, or a dungeon to dive straight in.


**L57**

> Text-grammar pool for the opening scene.


**L59**

> How the rest of the party keeps up with whoever is leading.


**L62**

> Beyond this many tiles, a follower hurries.


**L64**

> Steps a hurrying follower takes in one go.


**L69**

> Flags set on a new game.


**L71**

> Ends the game when it becomes true.


**L74**

> What finishing an `isEnding` arc does.


**L79**

> A mod this game expects, pinned to the exact build it was authored against.


**L83**

> The build this game was authored against.


**L86**

> Forced on, and play is blocked when it is missing.


**L88**

> Shown when the mod is missing or drifted — the author's own words.


**L97**

> Format version, so a future reader knows how to migrate this document.


**L101**

> Engine range this module expects, e.g. `^1.0.0`.


**L103**

> Base module to layer on, as `id@version`.


**L109**

> Mods this game expects.


**L120**

> A module as authored, before compilation resolves and indexes it.


**L126**

> Every addressable collection, as `section.collection` paths.


**L175**

> `narrative.memory` is deliberately absent: it is a settings block edited as one section rather


**L176**

> than a collection of independently addressable entries, and its `rules` list is nested inside


**L177**

> it.


### packages/module/src/schema/narrative.ts


**L1**

> Narrative: the text grammar, dialogue, and quests.


**L10**

> One way of saying something.


**L13**

> Supports `{placeholder}` interpolation resolved against the scene scope.


**L16**

> Say it differently once they know you, or fear you.


**L23**

> A named pool of phrasings.


**L32**

> One thing the player can say.


**L39**

> Shown greyed out with this reason instead of hidden.


**L42**

> A persuasion or intimidation attempt: the NPC resists.


**L46**

> The number to beat, as a formula.


**L48**

> The NPC's own roll opposes it, rather than a fixed number.


**L56**

> Selectable once, ever.


**L58**

> Omit to end the conversation.


**L66**

> What the NPC says; picked from a pool so repeat visits vary.


**L68**

> Runs when the node is first reached.


**L70**

> Reaching this node is itself remembered, so later talk can refer back.


**L72**

> Skip to another node when the party already qualifies.


**L92**

> A `goto` into nowhere would strand the player mid-conversation.


**L104**

> Every branch an option can take must land somewhere real.


**L122**

> One step of a quest.


**L127**

> Common shapes, so the usual objective needs no hand-written predicate.


**L129**

> Target of the objective, interpreted per `kind`.


**L132**

> For `custom`, the completion condition, and required.


**L134**

> Additional gate before the objective becomes active.


**L136**

> Hidden objectives do not appear in the journal until complete.


**L149**

> Becomes available when this holds.


**L151**

> Starts automatically rather than needing to be accepted.


**L154**

> Gate on development, items, factions, memory, or other quests.


**L156**

> Stages run in order; each holds its own objectives.


**L167**

> Prose shown while this stage is the active one.


**L174**

> Objectives must be completed in order.


**L179**

> Fails the quest when this becomes true, e.g. the giver dies.


**L195**

> Quests unlocked on completion, forming a chain.


**L197**

> What the world remembers about this, and who tells whom.


**L199**

> Can be taken again once finished.


**L201**

> Fails automatically this many in-world days after starting.


**L206**

> A quest with neither stages nor objectives can never be completed.


**L223**

> One thing the party can come to know.


**L227**

> The clue itself, in the words the player reads it in.


**L229**

> Longer context, when a single line is not enough.


**L232**

> Vary the wording instead of fixing it, for entries a player may meet more than one way.


**L234**

> Where it came from — "Netmender Ossa, Sarnport", "cut into the lintel".


**L239**

> A heading several lore entries hang under, so a partial answer reads as one.


**L245**

> In reading order, not discovery order.


**L250**

> A story arc: a gated sequence of quests.


**L257**

> Reaching this ends the game.


**L262**

> How a deed is weighted when it reaches a faction's ears.


**L268**

> Standing change for the faction that cares, before witness decay.


**L270**

> Which faction is affected; omit to derive it from the victim.


**L272**

> How memorable it is, as a multiplier on decay.


**L274**

> How much the story mutates as it spreads, 0..1.


**L281**

> How many nodes a dialogue may auto-advance through before it stops.


**L284**

> What the engine itself says: refusals, combat lines, narration fragments.


**L289**

> What the party can find out, and the headings it hangs under.


**L293**

> Memory, gossip, forgetting and learning.


### packages/module/src/schema/requirement.test.ts


**L7**

> Parse through the schema so defaults are applied, as a real module would.


**L14**

> A party member and world state to evaluate gates against.


**L28**

> Supplied by the engine's `buildScope`: the rank a tier starts at, and each faction's own ladder.


**L80**

> An untrained skill must read as rank 0, not as an error.


**L85**

> lockpicking is rank 3, and `adept` starts at rank 3.


**L88**

> lore is rank 1 — trained, but not yet adept.


**L133**

> An unknown faction reads as neutral rather than failing.


**L138**

> Standing with the wardens is 20: past `friend` (10), short of `trusted` (25).


**L169**

> The deed was on day 4 and it is now day 9.


**L175**

> "Requirements (lack of, or completed)" — absence is as common as presence.


**L199**

> The door opened by the key *or* by picking the lock.


### packages/module/src/schema/requirement.ts


**L1**

> Requirements — the one gating vocabulary.


**L8**

> Quest states a requirement can test.


**L14**

> Minimum rank.


**L16**

> Named tier from `rules.masteryTiers`, if the module uses them.


**L25**

> Consume the item when the gate opens, as a key is spent.


**L27**

> Must be worn or wielded, not merely carried.


**L36**

> Require a specific objective rather than the whole quest.


**L46**

> Named rank from the faction's own ladder.


**L51**

> A memory condition.


**L55**

> Whose memory to inspect; defaults to the NPC in the conversation.


**L57**

> False tests that they have not heard about it.


**L59**

> Only count deeds within this many in-world days.


**L67**

> Omit to test merely that the flag is set.


**L72**

> Something the party has found out.


**L88**

> The clauses shared by a requirement and by each of its alternatives.


**L95**

> Character development: which classes and ancestries qualify.


**L102**

> What the actor is — "this only affects undead", "this line only appears if you speak Dwarvish".


**L107**

> Coin in the party's purse — what makes a toll gate a toll.


**L115**

> `.optional()` where its neighbours default to `[]`, so no module hash moves.


**L118**

> Conditions that must NOT hold — "only before you have met her".


**L127**

> `.optional()` for the hash reason given on the positive clause above.


**L133**

> Escape hatch for anything the structured clauses cannot express.


**L137**

> One alternative branch.


**L140**

> A gate.


**L151**

> Alternatives are absent on a branch, so treat that uniformly.


**L156**

> Read a clause list defensively.


**L161**

> The `without` block, likewise, may be missing entirely.


**L183**

> True when nothing is gated, so callers can skip evaluation entirely.


**L217**

> Lower a requirement to a DSL predicate.


**L226**

> Class and ancestry lists are alternatives: any of them qualifies.


**L248**

> A tier is a named rung on the rank ladder, per `masteryTier.atRank`.


**L262**

> Every named tongue must be spoken, not merely one of them.


**L283**

> Ranks are thresholds on the faction's own standing, scoped per faction.


**L295**

> `memory.<who>.<deedKind>` is how the engine exposes what is remembered.


**L305**

> `lore.<id>` is the minute it was learned, so `exists` is the whole test.


**L311**

> Negations.


### packages/module/src/schema/rules.ts


**L1**

> The ruleset, defined entirely in data.


**L22**

> A character attribute.


**L39**

> A depletable pool such as hit points or focus.


**L45**

> Evaluated with the character in scope, e.g. class die + endurance per level.


**L48**

> Starting value; defaults to full.


**L50**

> Fraction of max restored by a rest, 0..1.


**L53**

> Reaching min triggers these, which is how death is expressed.


**L58**

> A stat computed from attributes and gear, e.g. Guard, Initiative, Speed.


**L77**

> How a second application of the same condition behaves.


**L85**

> Which way a roll leans.


**L95**

> Omitted means it lasts until removed.


**L98**

> Runs once per round; this is how poison and bleeding tick.


**L101**

> Additive modifiers to derived stats while active.


**L103**

> Actions the condition forbids, e.g. `stunned` blocking `action`.


**L105**

> Senses this shuts off, e.g. `blinded` closing `sight`.


**L107**

> Which way the dice lean while this condition is applied.


**L117**

> Whether being under this hides who you are, so a witness is less likely to name you.


**L119**

> A save each round to shake it off.


**L124**

> When the save is made: on application, at end of turn, or both.


**L129**

> Conditions this one supersedes, e.g. unconscious implying prone.


**L135**

> How a check resolves.


**L141**

> What happens when a roll is handed more than one swing.


**L143**

> Natural roll at or above this is a critical success; null disables crits.


**L146**

> Which kinds of roll a critical and a fumble can happen on.


**L150**

> What a critical hit multiplies damage by: the whole amount, modifier included.


**L152**

> What a successful save against `onSuccess: "half"` leaves, as a fraction.


**L154**

> The floor a passive score is measured from: `passiveBase + modifier`.


**L156**

> What "opposed" means when one side is only resisting.


**L158**

> Which way a scaled damage number is rounded — resistance, a save for half, a critical.


**L160**

> Which way a reputation spill is rounded.


**L162**

> What a weapon attack adds, when the ruleset wants more than the bare attribute modifier.


**L165**

> Named difficulties content can refer to instead of raw numbers.


**L174**

> Applied on reaching this level.


**L179**

> What a level beyond the first adds to the vital resource, on top of `rules.resources[].max`.


**L192**

> Proficiency-style bonus by level, if the module uses one.


**L194**

> The rank a bare `skillProficiencies` entry grants.


**L204**

> What a turn is made of.


**L210**

> Uses available per turn.


**L220**

> In world-clock minutes.


**L222**

> Which restore fraction on resources applies.


**L225**

> Chance of being interrupted while in a dangerous place.


**L230**

> A named rung on the skill ladder — novice, adept, master.


**L236**

> Skill rank at which this tier is reached.


**L241**

> Equipment slots, so gear layout is a module decision.


**L242**

> A named quality a weapon can have: finesse, versatile, two-handed, thrown.


**L248**

> Additive modifiers to derived stats while a weapon with this is wielded.


**L250**

> Attributes a weapon with this property may attack with, on top of the one the ability names.


**L256**

> What money is called here.


**L257**

> How far the party can reach without moving.


**L260**

> Chebyshev tiles you can hold a conversation across.


**L262**

> Tiles you can pick something up from, or hand it over.


**L267**

> Searching a room, and defusing what it turns up.


**L270**

> How far a search reaches.


**L272**

> How close you must be to disarm what you found.


**L281**

> Whether the purse can go below zero.


**L290**

> How many items fit, e.g. two rings.


**L310**

> The tactical layer.


**L323**

> What creatures do when nothing is telling them what to do.


**L325**

> Resource consumed when a character is reduced to zero, e.g. `hp`.


**L328**

> Attribute that breaks initiative ties, if any.


**L330**

> Default size for creatures that do not declare one.


**L332**

> How a creature moves when it declares no speeds, and how generation reads connectivity.


**L336**

> How an NPC's signed `disposition` becomes a stance toward the party.


**L342**

> Lowest disposition in this band.

> A kind of ground.

> Single character used to draw it on a text map.

> Suggested colour when drawn on a text map.

> Blocks line of sight, and therefore field of view and witnessing.

> Tiles of movement consumed to enter.

> Only creatures with one of these movement modes may enter.


**L33**

> Cover granted to a creature standing behind this.

> Fires when a creature enters — lava, caltrops, a pressure plate.

> Fires at the end of a turn spent standing here.

> Light emitted, in tiles.

> A door-like terrain that a gate can be attached to.

> How a space is drawn.

> Terrain laid over the floor, either sprinkled or in patches.

> Fraction of the open floor this covers.

> `patch` only: tiles across a typical blob.

> `patch` only: 1 gives smooth outlines, 3 a ragged coast.

> Laid in a band around a patch — a shore, a verge, a scree slope.

> Lower is laid first, and a later entry never overwrites it.

> Fills the space beyond the walls, e.g. rock, void, open sky.

> A position on a map.

> How a space is sized and drawn.

> Dice notation, so size varies per instance.

> A hand-built map used exactly as authored.

> Hand-authored tiles, as rows of palette glyphs.

> Maps a glyph in `layout` to a terrain, beyond the palette's own.

> A ragged layout would silently produce a jagged map; rows must be equal.

> Static maps: hand-authored, identical across seeds.

> Which collection each layer kind's cells resolve against.

> Names the layer in the editor and its CSV file on disk.

> Every layer must be the same rectangle.

> The base terrain layer makes the tiles total; without one there is no ground to stand on.

> — direction ——————————————————————————————————————————————

> — rolls ——————————————————————————————————————————————————

> Every key the engine can ask for.

> Lookup by key, built once.

> Keys a document must declare: the pieces other messages are built from.

> The canonical wording, ready to write into a module document.

> The smallest `systemText` block a module can compile with.

> What the engine says, keyed.


### packages/module/src/schema/tactical.ts


**L1**

> The tactical layer: saves, resistances, slots, senses, movement and opportunities.


**L7**

> A saving throw.


**L14**

> Difficulty when nothing sets one explicitly.


**L20**

> A creature size, carrying the numbers that depend on it.


**L25**

> Space occupied, in feet or whatever unit the module uses.


**L27**

> Natural reach before weapons.

> Multiplier on carrying capacity.

> Hit-die step, for modules that scale HP by size.

> Beast, undead, aberration — used by spells and features that target a type.

> A way of noticing things: sight, hearing, smell, tremorsense.

> Default range when a creature has it without one specified.

> Ignores these conditions when perceiving, e.g. blindsight ignoring darkness.

> `line` travels straight and needs an unobstructed path — sight, and sound.

> What stops it.

> `cliff` is full strength to the edge of range and nothing beyond.

> Minutes a trace left on the ground stays perceptible.

> How fast this sense travels, in tiles per minute.

> Signal left at the far edge of reach, once it has spread that far.

> Minutes a creature remembers having perceived something.

> Text grammar keys for a strong and a faint impression from this sense.

> What stopping to use this sense reads like when it turns nothing up.

> Signal needed to notice at all, to go and look, and to fight.

> How a creature is moving, and therefore what it gives off.

> Multiplies movement allowance: creeping is slow, running is not.

> What this stance gives off to each sense, as a multiplier.

> Skill whose ranks quieten this stance further.

> Emission removed per rank of that skill.

> How perception behaves overall: which sense draws the map, and how long curiosity lasts.

> The sense used to draw the map and describe a room.

> How long a creature keeps looking for something it noticed.

> Stance a creature uses when it has chosen none.

> The least a creature can ever give off, whatever its stance or skill.

> How many traces one tile keeps, per sense; the oldest fall off.

> What a creature does when nobody is telling it what to do.

> How far from where it was placed it will wander, in module units.

> How far from that same spot a lead may pull it.


**L148**

> How far it will chase before turning for home.


**L151**

> Odds it moves at all on a given idle step.


**L154**

> Rounds it stays in a fight after nobody can perceive anybody.


**L157**

> Multipliers on how fast it moves for each reason it moves.


**L168**

> Which senses it acts on, best first.


**L171**

> Whether a trace left on the ground is worth following.


**L174**

> Whose presence it registers at all.


**L180**

> The same thing as a per-creature override, with every field genuinely absent.


**L204**

> Walk, fly, swim, burrow, climb.


**L209**

> Speed when a creature has the mode but no explicit rate.


**L211**

> Difficult terrain and similar costs.


**L213**

> Falls when the mode is lost, e.g. flight while unconscious.


**L225**

> Rare languages gate dialogue and readable content.

> How damage of a type is modified for a creature.

> Ignored when the damage carries one of these tags, e.g. silvered.

> Spellcasting.

> Highest spell level the module uses.

> Slots per caster level: `slotTable["3"] = [4, 2]` is four 1st and two 2nd.

> Resource spent when `mode` includes points.

> Point cost per spell level, indexed by level.

> Spell save DC and attack bonus, as formulas over the caster.

> Casting again while concentrating drops the first spell.

> Save made when damaged while concentrating.

> Usually max(10, half the damage taken).

> Concentrating on more than one spell at a time.

> Recovered on which rest kinds.

> Which action a spell component is, so silence and manacles work.

> Reactions triggered by someone else's action — opportunity attacks, shield, counterspell.

> What provokes it.

> Action type it consumes, usually a reaction.

> Ability used to respond; omit for a plain weapon attack.

> Uses per round, across all provocations.


**L317**

> Cover, and anything else that adjusts a roll situationally.

> Bonus to the defender's guard-equivalent.


**L324**

> Blocks line of effect entirely.

> The section rendered from the `SYSTEM_TEXT` registry rather than the shape.


**L20**

> The four shapes the behaviour language takes, named rather than lumped.


**L23**

> A field's type, shaped so Markdown and JSX can each render it their own way.

> Schemas that mean something specific, matched by object identity.

> Canonical dotted path, e.g. `content.npcs.gullibility`.

> The schema default, or `undefined` when the field has none.

> Collection this field points at, when it is a reference.

> The sentence a reference field carries after its collection, if any.

> Section path this field's own fields live under, when it is an object.

> Dotted path, `''` for the document itself.

> Owning section path, `null` for the document itself.

> `content.npcs.shop` reads as `['content', 'npcs', 'shop']`.

> True for `narrative.systemText`, whose two hundred keys come from the `SYSTEM_TEXT` registry.


**L85**

> Set when the chain passed through a schema in `NAMED`.


**L89**

> Strip `optional`/`nullable`/`default`/`effects` down to the real type.


**L123**

> The object branches of a union, in declaration order.


**L133**

> The object a field is built from, looking through arrays and unions.


**L149**

> Object schema to the first path that reached it.


**L184**

> Every branch an object of the same shape bar a discriminating literal.


**L195**

> A lazy schema that is not one of the four named DSL shapes.


**L222**

> Claim a section for an object field as it is scanned, so siblings share one.


**L256**

> Every field path the walk produces, in document order.
